**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Kay mentions seeing something red from under the cherry tree (Testimony 3). This implies that Kay was near the cherry tree on the viewing platform when she observed something red. However, Evidence 7 describes the layout of the viewing platform, stating that the cherry tree is located at the left-most edge, while the Candy Stand is at the right-most edge. Testimony 4 states that Kay saw someone walking towards her from the other side of the Candy Stand, suggesting she was closer to the Candy Stand rather than the cherry tree.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the specific locations of the cherry tree and the Candy Stand on the viewing platform.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Since no propositions directly apply, we proceed to step 4.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises because Kay's recollection of seeing something red from under the cherry tree (left-most edge) conflicts with her statement of seeing someone walking towards her from the other side of the Candy Stand (right-most edge). These two statements cannot both be true simultaneously given the described layout of the viewing platform.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the propositions do apply. According to White's testimony, Mia Fey ran to the left and was struck from the right, implying she would fall backward (if hit from the front). However, the autopsy report does not specify the direction of the fall, but it does indicate that the blow was instantaneous and fatal. If we assume that the killer struck Mia Fey from behind while she was running to the left, she would likely fall forward, which contradicts the expected behavior based on Proposition 3.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Luke Atmey states that he put his back to the wall to fight, and the thief's blow landed upon his third eye. This implies that Luke Atmey had his back to Mask☆DeMasque when he was hit. 

On the other hand, Evidence 4 describes the Sacred Urn being found in Atmey's Office and having pink splotches all over it. Evidence 21 mentions that the Urn Box has pink paint stains on it, and Evidence 22 explains that these stains came from when Adrian Andrews dropped the urn's box two weeks ago. 

However, the spatial contradiction arises when we consider the spatial proposition related to visibility and positioning.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Spatial Propositions 1, 2, 4, and 7 are relevant to the spatial relationship between characters and objects.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 7 states: "If Luke Atmey has his back to Mask☆DeMasque then Luke Atmey cannot see Mask☆DeMasque."

This proposition directly applies to the contradiction because if Luke Atmey had his back to Mask☆DeMasque, he would not have been able to see the thief. Yet, the presence of the Sacred Urn in Atmey's Office suggests that Mask☆DeMasque was there, and Atmey's testimonies imply he was aware of the thief's actions.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 7 does apply, revealing the contradiction. Luke Atmey's testimony about putting his back to the wall contradicts the fact that Mask☆DeMasque was able to enter his office and leave the Sacred Urn there, suggesting that Atmey should have seen the thief or at least been aware of his presence.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 4, "testimony": 5}

  "testimony": 5

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

- Testimony 0 states that the victim used her keycard and entered the Committee chamber with the culprit.
- Evidence 19 mentions blood was detected on the secret elevator, which looks like it dripped through to the floor below (the meeting room).
- Evidence 20 describes a clothes box in which the body was hidden, with traces of blood inside.
- Evidence 21 indicates blood found in the Committee chamber, suggesting it might have been placed there to hide the existence of the storeroom.

There seems to be a spatial contradiction regarding the location of the bloodstains and the body. According to Testimony 0, the crime involving the stabbing took place in the Committee chamber, yet Evidence 19 suggests that blood dripped from the secret elevator to the meeting room below, indicating that the crime might have initially occurred in the storeroom above.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

- Prop 4: If the blood on the secret elevator drips through to the floor below and the floor below is the meeting room then the blood on the secret elevator is inside the meeting room.
- Prop 1: If the secret elevator is locked then the secret elevator cannot be passed through.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- Prop 4 is directly relevant because it suggests that if blood dripped from the secret elevator to the meeting room, it would imply that the blood originated from the storeroom above, where the secret elevator is located. This contradicts Testimony 0, which states that the victim and the culprit entered the Committee chamber and the crime took place there.
- Prop 1 is indirectly relevant because if the secret elevator is locked, it would mean that the blood could not have dripped from the storeroom to the meeting room, reinforcing the idea that the bloodstain in the meeting room could not have come from the storeroom.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between the storeroom and the meeting room. If the blood on the secret elevator dripped through to the meeting room, it implies that the initial crime scene was in the storeroom, not the Committee chamber as stated in Testimony 0. This contradicts the claim that the victim and the culprit entered the Committee chamber and the crime took place there.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 19, "testimony": 0}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Ini Miney states that she was in the passenger's seat and that her sister Mimi Miney was driving the car. According to Evidence 6 (Newspaper Clipping 2), Mimi Miney died because she fell asleep at the wheel, causing the car to crash into the center divide. However, Ini Miney also mentions that she opened the right door to exit the car after the crash.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction. Propositions 1, 2, 3, and 4 are particularly relevant here.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if Ini Miney is in the passenger's seat, she cannot be driving the car. This aligns with Ini's testimony that she was in the passenger's seat and Mimi was driving.

Proposition 2 states that if Mimi Miney is driving the car, she cannot be in the passenger's seat. This also aligns with Ini's testimony.

Proposition 3 states that if Ini Miney is to the right of the car's center divide, she is to the right of Mimi Miney. Since Ini Miney opened the right door to exit the car, she must have been to the right of the car's center divide, implying she was to the right of Mimi Miney.

Proposition 4 states that if Ini Miney is in the car and the car crashes into the center divide, Ini Miney cannot be outside the car at the time of impact. This proposition does not directly contradict Ini's statement since she mentioned waking up in flames and escaping, which implies she was inside initially and then outside.

However, Proposition 3 reveals a potential contradiction. If Ini Miney was in the passenger's seat and opened the right door, she would be to the right of Mimi Miney, which suggests Mimi Miney was driving from the left side. This contradicts the typical setup of a car in most countries, where the driver's seat is on the left side and the passenger's seat is on the right side.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 3 applies and helps us identify the contradiction. The spatial arrangement described by Ini Miney (being in the passenger's seat and opening the right door) implies that Mimi Miney was driving from the left side, which is consistent with standard car setups. However, the contradiction arises from the fact that Mimi Miney, according to Ini, was driving a British car, which typically has the driver's seat on the right side. Therefore, if Ini was in the passenger's seat (which would be on the left side in a British car), she would not be able to open the right door to exit the car.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Spatial Proposition Prop 2 states: "If Elise Deauxnim has her back to the Stone Lantern then Elise Deauxnim cannot see the Stone Lantern."

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition Prop 2 applies directly to the situation described in Iris's testimonies. According to Iris, Elise had her back to the Stone Lantern, which means she could not see the Stone Lantern based on Prop 2.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises because the spatial proposition (Prop 2) confirms that having one's back to an object means they cannot see it. Since Iris testified that Elise had her back to the Stone Lantern, this implies Elise could not see the Stone Lantern, which is consistent with the proposition. However, the critical point is that the Stone Lantern was not lit the night of the crime (as per Evidence 20), meaning the area around it would be dark (Prop 10). If Elise could not see the Stone Lantern due to her position, and the area around the Stone Lantern was dark, it raises questions about how Iris could observe the exact positioning of Elise relative to the Stone Lantern in such conditions.

- Prop 1: If right-hand fingerprints of Phoenix Wright are on the Deadly Bottle then Phoenix Wright held the Deadly Bottle with the right hand.

This proposition suggests that if Phoenix Wright's fingerprints are on the Deadly Bottle, he held it with his right hand. However, the description of the Deadly Bottle indicates that the prints are upside-down, which would be inconsistent with holding the bottle normally with the right hand.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. The spatial reasoning is that if Phoenix Wright's fingerprints were placed upside-down on the Deadly Bottle, it suggests that he did not simply grab the bottle from the table as Olga described, but rather picked it up in a way that resulted in his fingers touching the bottle in an inverted position.

This proposition supports the idea that the victim was indeed found inside the lantern in the reception hall, but it doesn't resolve the contradiction regarding whether he fell into the lantern or was placed there.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed propositions directly address the contradiction between Ema's testimony and the Photo of Victim. The contradiction arises from the spatial reasoning that if Dumas Gloomsbury was hit from behind and fell into the lantern, he would have fallen forward according to Proposition 1. ## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Shih-na's testimonies indicate that she was in the next room over when she heard a scream (Testimony 2). Additionally, she mentions that she was searching for the Yatagarasu in Babahl (Testimony 1). This suggests that she was in the Babahl side of the embassy when the scream was heard.

However, Evidence 29 states that the Revolving Fireplace Wall on Babahl's side has been used, and the Yatagarasu escaped through it into the next room over. This implies that the Yatagarasu was initially in Babahl and then moved to the next room, which is Allebahst.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Proposition 4 states: "If the Revolving Fireplace Wall is used and Shih-na is in the next room, then Shih-na is far from the Yatagarasu."

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 4 applies here. According to Shih-na's testimonies, she was in the next room over when the scream was heard, implying she was in Allebahst. However, the Revolving Fireplace Wall being used indicates that the Yatagarasu was in Babahl and then moved to Allebahst. Proposition 4 suggests that if the Yatagarasu used the Revolving Fireplace Wall to move rooms, Shih-na would be far from the Yatagarasu, meaning she could not have been in the same room as the Yatagarasu when the scream was heard. This contradicts the implication that Shih-na was in the next room over (Allebahst) when the Yatagarasu was in Babahl and then moved to Allebahst.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial movement described by the use of the Revolving Fireplace Wall and Shih-na's claim of being in the next room over when she heard the scream. If the Yatagarasu was in Babahl and used the wall to move to Allebahst, Shih-na, who was in Allebahst, would be far from the Yatagarasu, making it impossible for her to hear the scream from the next room over.
